# CI 4617 — U4 Anti-Duplicate Guard / Auto-Landing Disconnect

- **Lane**: `bridge-20261028-u4-dupguard-race` (solver on self-filed finding #1990, P3-low)
- **Discovered**: 2026-10-28 cycle 13:20+08 (Job 478330), while sweeping board truth after a loop "clean" verdict
- **Verdict**: PR-lane failure with **zero live damage** but a **structural enforceability gap** in the U4 (no-duplicate-PR) invariant: the Anti-Duplicate PR Guard failed on PR #1979's pipeline run and the very same run's Autonomous Auto-Landing job executed the squash-merge anyway, because `duplicate-pr-guard` is absent from the landing job's `needs` list.
- **Disposition**: report-only single additive doc (this file). The guard, the landing job, and any workflow change live in `.github/workflows/pipeline.yml` — protected path, admin-gated per the #1769/#1822 precedent (re-affirmed by WO #1912 recert lane #1973). Nothing in this lane touches protected paths.

---

## 1. Verdict summary

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What failed | Job `👥 Anti-Duplicate PR Guard` (`duplicate-pr-guard`) in PR #1979's pipeline run #4617 (id 40636808713, event `pull_request`, head sha `59364cf`) |
| Failure line | `::error::Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #1916.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #1917 (branch: bridge-20261028-browser-webbotauth-probe)` at `2026-10-28T05:14:27.386Z` |
| Root cause (mechanical) | Two legitimate PRs carried `Closes #1916` in their bodies at the same moment (see §5); the guard's match rule flags any other **open** PR whose title/body contains `closes|fixes|resolves #N` for an issue this PR targets |
| Root cause (structural) | `duplicate-pr-guard` is **not in `auto-landing`'s `needs`** — a guard failure cannot block the squash-merge (§4). The failing run landed the PR itself |
| Was the flagged competitor real? | **No collision of deliverables.** #1917 (satellite-lane vehicle, RFC 9421 web-bot-auth probe) and #1979 (watchdog FP fix) are different works sharing one WO number; both lanes completed green (§5, §6) |
| Repo damage | None. #1979 landed as 9e6bbc9; post-merge main run 4618 (push, 9e6bbc9) = success; #1916's actual fixer landed and the live reclassification (`SATELLITE_TARGET`, risk=0) is on main |
| Fix ownership | Admin-gated (workflow source). Agent-actionable part = this receipt + the hardening proposals in §7 |

Why no auto-mirror exists for this failure: the failure-ingress dispatch ("Trigger Federation CI Watchdog on Failure") is a step inside the `auto-landing` job gated `if: failure()` — the landing job **succeeded** in run 4617 (it merged), so the run-level failure (the guard) produced no dispatch, no issue, no WO. This report is the only receipt.

## 3. Guard anatomy (pipeline.yml, `duplicate-pr-guard`, L305–367)

The job is gated `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'` (L307). Its detection logic, verbatim semantics:

1. **Escape hatch**: `guard-exempt` label skips the job.
2. **Own targets**: `TARGET_ISSUES` = explicit `Closes|Fixes|Resolves #N` matches in the PR's own title/body; `BRANCH_ISSUES` = `issue-N` tokens in the branch name; fallback to any `#N` reference if both are empty.
3. **Competitor scan**: `gh pr list --state open --json number,title,body,headRefName` — a **live snapshot** of every other open PR at execution time.
4. **Match rule** per target issue `#N`: competitor's title/body contains `closes|fixes|resolves #N` (whole-token, the #120 fix) **OR** competitor's branch name contains digit token `N` with non-digit boundaries. First match wins; `exit 1`.

Two properties matter for this finding: the match surface includes **plain closing references in other PRs' bodies** (semantic-free — it cannot distinguish a true fixer from a lane-bookkeeping closer), and the whole check is a **point-in-time snapshot** (the verdict is only as fresh as the listing).

## 4. Auto-landing anatomy — the disconnect (pipeline.yml, `auto-landing`, L794–806)

```yaml
auto-landing:
  name: "🛬 Autonomous Auto-Landing"
  needs: [branch-naming-guard, claim-guard, boundary_sanitization_guard,
          test-matrix, secrets-scan, mcp-tower, studio-verification]
  if: |
    always() &&
    github.event_name == 'pull_request' &&
    github.event.pull_request.draft == false &&
    !contains(needs.*.result, 'failure') &&
    !contains(needs.*.result, 'cancelled')
  concurrency:
    group: landing-merge-open
    cancel-in-progress: false
```

- **`duplicate-pr-guard` is not in `needs`.** The landing gate evaluates exactly seven jobs; a `failure` result from the duplicate guard never appears in `needs.*.result`, so `!contains(..., 'failure')` stays true and the merge proceeds. In run 4617 the landing job did not merely "tolerate" the guard failure — it executed the squash-merge inside the same failing run.
- **Event-surface asymmetry (secondary hole)**: the landing job itself only runs on `pull_request` events, but the pipeline also triggers on `push` events for bridge branches (L105 gate). A push-event run of the same sha is guard-free **and** lander-free — harmless by itself, but it means every PR lane normally produces two parallel verdict planes for one commit: a guard-bearing PR-event run and a guard-free push-event run (see the concurrency analysis below). Any consumer of "green per sha" that does not distinguish the planes inherits the asymmetry.
- **Concurrency groups guarantee the parallel planes** (L64–66): `group: ci-open-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.event.pull_request.number || github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.event_name == 'pull_request' }}`. A push run groups by `github.ref`, a PR run groups by PR number — **disjoint groups**, so a branch push and its PR event never cancel each other and the same head sha runs twice, concurrently, by construction. That is the race window builder; run pair 4616/4617 (3 s apart) is it live.
- **Contrast with the claim gate**: `claim-guard` IS in `needs` — the atomic-claim invariant (U5-family) gates the merge, the anti-duplicate invariant (U4) does not. The asymmetry looks like an oversight rather than a design choice, which is exactly why it is worth a receipt.

## 5. The collision anatomy: one WO, two legitimate closers

- **WO #1916** was the bridge mirror of the landing-watchdog's live false positive: the watchdog classified the `browser-webbotauth` satellite lane's WO as `LANDING_AT_RISK` even though the lane lands in `Supremeai-bd/supremeai-browser`, outside the open-repo history/PR planes. PR #1979's deliverable (a new `SATELLITE_TARGET` verdict with on-demand Target Repo fetch, fail-closed) is the **fixer** of #1916; its body's Linked Work Order section carries `Closes #1916` (bridge `originalIssueNumber` wiring), and its landing reclassified the live WO (`risk=0`, `LANDING_AT_RISK=0`).
- **PR #1917** is the satellite lane's open-repo **vehicle** (zero-new-dep Ed25519 web-bot-auth probe for radar R20/#1675). Its body — bridge-generated in the same convention — carries `Closes #1675 Refs #1675 Closes #1916 Refs #1916`: the lane collects the Closes of every WO associated with it, including #1916, whose subject was *this very lane's* watchdog false positive. From the guard's point of view, #1917's body is a `closes #1916` match.
- At 05:14:27Z the guard's snapshot listed #1917 as open and matched it against #1979's target `#1916` → `exit 1`. Neither PR was a duplicate of the other; the collision is a **shared-WO bookkeeping artifact** of the Closes-wiring convention. As more WOs accumulate multi-lane associations (watchdog mirrors, recert lanes, closeout sweeps — e.g. PR #1982's Closes of two issues in one doc), this false-duplicate class will recur.
- Outcome was benign: both lanes completed green, #1979 landed and its landing is exactly what closed out #1916's live risk. The damage model is therefore **noise + enforceability hole**, not corruption: a *real* duplicate would sail through the identical path (§4), and honest lanes get red runs (and spurious "duplicate" stickers in their CI history) when a lane-mate shares a WO.

## 6. Honest open point: the #1917 open-sighting vs merged ground truth

Proven: at 05:14:27Z the guard's `gh pr list --state open` returned #1917; the page now reads **State--merged**; no #1917 commit exists in any open-repo branch under any merge strategy; the page's last visible relative-time is 04:11:34Z. Unresolved: whether the sighting was (a) a stale open-listing under fleet pressure (hundreds of PR events per hour keep the pulls listing hot), or (b) a state transition of a tower-bookkeeping-managed vehicle PR inside the 05:14→05:2x window — vehicle PRs for satellite lanes "land outside the open-repo history/PR planes" (the #1916 `SATELLITE_TARGET` reclassification says exactly this), so their open/merged state may be maintained by the tower's bookkeeping rather than by a git merge in this repo. Both candidates are consistent with every receipt; the guard's `::error` output records only number/title/branch of the competitor — **no `state`, no `updated_at`** — which is precisely why the forensics dead-end here. §7 proposes the metadata fix. No verdict in this report depends on resolving (a) vs (b): even under (b) (sighting momentarily true), the §4 disconnect means the guard's failure still could not and did not block the merge.

## 6a. Post-merge live reproduction (same hour): this finding's own PR re-tripped the guard

The original lane's deliverable (PR #1996, filed as #1990's receipt) became a second, independent live reproduction within 28 minutes of the original event — with a **third collision variant** the original run could not show:

- **Run 4654** (id 40639585119, event `pull_request`, PR #1996's lane, ~05:42:59Z): `duplicate-pr-guard` FAILED with `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #1675.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #1917 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-browser-webbotauth-probe)`.
- **Mechanism (variant F3' — quote-capture)**: the PR's evidence block quoted the collision anatomy of §5, including the verbatim body section of PR #1917. The guard's TARGET_ISSUES extraction scans the PR's own title/body for `(closes|fixes|resolves) #N` — it cannot distinguish a closing reference from a **mention of one inside quoted evidence**. The quoted string imported target #1675 into this PR's target set; the competitor scan then matched the persistently-listed #1917. Report-type lanes that quote guard evidence (the #1925-class triage receipts do exactly this) are structurally prone to the same self-trip.
- **Second #1917 open-sighting** at 05:42:59Z, 28 minutes after the first: the "post-check state transition" reading of §6 candidate (b) is now strongly disfavored — the listing plane persistently reports #1917 open while its page renders the merged badge. Whether that is a long-TTL listing cache or tower-managed vehicle-PR state diverging from the git planes, it is a **stable, reproducible divergence**, not a one-second race, which sharpens §7 item 2 (record `state`/`updated_at` in the guard error) from nice-to-have to the minimum diagnosability bar.
- **The landing proceeded anyway** — PR #1996 merged as 5b05769 (05:43:29Z) with the guard failure sitting in the same run's history. Second live proof of the §4 disconnect within one hour, this time on the reporting lane itself: push runs 4652/4653 green, pull_request run 4654 red on the guard, merge executed regardless. The CI history of #1996 is public and self-contained; no reconstruction needed.
- **Agent-side mitigation — first attempt REFUTED in-flight; refined model below.** The first addendum (a2, PR #2006) shipped a paraphrase rule claiming safety for any body that avoids a closing verb adjacent to a `#N` token, with its own guard-green run pledged as proof. The proof ran and REFUTED the claim: run 4678 (id 40640406168, pull_request, ~05:51:31Z) failed with the identical `targets #1675` / competitor #1917 signature even though the a2 body contained **zero** closing-verb adjacencies (verified by grep over the submission fields). The a2 PR body carries `Refs #1990 Refs #2005` — the bridge's convention for still-open findings wires **Refs, not Closes** — so `TARGET_ISSUES ∪ BRANCH_ISSUES` was empty and the guard's **fallback branch fired: every `#N` token anywhere in the title/body becomes a target**. The a2 body's error quotation ("...already targets #1675...") was therefore captured by the fallback, no closing verb required.
- **Refined guard model (source + live A/B)**: (1) targets = explicit closing statements in own title/body ∪ issue-N branch tokens; (2) if both are empty — which is guaranteed for every Refs-wired PR, i.e. the bridge's default for report lanes against still-open findings — the extraction **falls back to all `#N` references in the body**; (3) each target is then matched against other open PRs via closing-verb adjacency OR branch digit-token; (4) failure does not block landing (§4). Consequence: **any Refs-wired PR whose body mentions a number that an open competitor targets will trip the guard, regardless of quoting hygiene.** The F3' class is broader than quote-capture: fallback-all-refs is the operative mechanism on this repo's dominant PR shape.
- **Refined agent-side defense (evidence-bearing, Refs-wired lanes)**: quoting hygiene is necessary but insufficient; the binding rule is **no bare `#N` token in the PR body may name anything an open competitor targets**. In practice: refer to competitor PRs and WOs as "the vehicle PR for the browser lane" or by run ids ("run 4654" — un-prefixed numbers are invisible to the `#[0-9]+` extraction), keep the verbatim `#N` strings inside files (the guard scans title/body only), and accept that the bridge's own `Refs #<issue> Refs <WO>` tail is safe because no open PR targets those numbers. This correction's own submission (a3) is the positive control: its body contains no `#`-prefixed competitor numbers, so a green guard run on it — against the red runs 4654/4678 — completes the A/B/A' triple (quote-capture red, fallback-only red, fallback-aware green) and pins the model end-to-end. The durable fixes stay admin-side (§7).

## 6b. Out-of-sample reproduction (run 4698): the §6a body-hygiene defense is falsified by the fleet's mandated PR shape

- **The event**: run 4698 (id 40642476341, event `pull_request`, head branch `bridge/bridge-20261028-d4-probe-first-live-pass`, 06:12:39Z) — the D4 fleet-contract probe lane, a report-only docs PR landed as 64ab152 via push run 4699 at 06:13:16Z, **37 seconds after** the guard verdict. `duplicate-pr-guard` FAILED: `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #1786.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #1787 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-lease-ssot-wiring)`. Fourth merge-despite-guard receipt (9e6bbc9, 5b05769, the a2 addendum lane, 64ab152) and the third distinct trip mechanism.
- **Mechanism under the §6a refined model**: the D4 PR was report-only — no closing statement (TARGET set empty) and no digit token in its branch name (BRANCH set empty) — so the **fallback-all-refs branch** fired and every `#N` mention in the body became a target. Its body contained an honest board-scan accounting line listing the board's in-gates work orders by number, among them the lease-SSOT work order that the still-open vehicle PR #1787 targets. One bare mention was sufficient; no guard-evidence quoting involved (this is not F3′).
- **What this proves and what it refutes — the distinction matters**: the refined model gains a clean **out-of-sample confirmation** (the trip was predictable from body content alone, before the log was pulled); the §6a **agent-side defense loses its sufficiency**. The tripped mention was not an author error — it is the contribution protocol's own **mandated anti-stampede audit trail**: a cycle must document why each unclaimed board item was left unclaimed, which requires naming open work orders, and every in-gates work order by definition has an open competitor PR targeting it. A defense that forbids naming open work orders in a PR body is **structurally incompatible with the fleet's standard PR shape** for any honest board-accounting cycle.
- **Consequence for §7**: the agent-side surface for this failure class is exhausted (the A/B/A′ triple in §6a plus this out-of-sample falsifier). Item 1 (wire the guard into `auto-landing`'s `needs`) remains the only lever with real coverage; item 3's lane-slug matching would additionally de-fang the entire mention class, since a body mention is no longer a competitor-match surface when matching is keyed on the `bridge-YYYYMMDD-slug` join token. Until then, every report lane that performs honest board accounting rolls the dice on a red guard run — a structural tax on the reporting fleet, not an author mistake.
- **Forecast for watchdogs**: four landings-despite-guard in ~58 minutes of observation (05:14Z, 05:43Z, ~05:51Z, 06:12Z), zero blocked merges, zero live damage — the disconnect is benign in effect but continuous in occurrence. Any CI-health signal keyed on guard failures will keep emitting noise until §7 item 1 lands.

## 6c. Receipt five (run 4725): the re-land collision — the guard vs the successor's own stale ancestor, and a hardening interaction hazard

- **The event**: run 4725 (id 40645409098, event `pull_request`, head branch `bridge/bridge-20261028-r42-docs-link-integrity-r3`, 06:41:47Z) — the R42 link-integrity gate re-land lane. `duplicate-pr-guard` FAILED: `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #1938.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #1954 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-r42-docs-link-integrity-guard)`. The re-land landed anyway as 0141166 within the same hour (main history). **Fifth merge-despite-guard receipt** (05:14Z, 05:43Z, ~05:51Z, 06:12Z, 06:41Z), still zero blocked merges and zero live damage.
- **Anatomy — a new axis for mechanism class (a)**: the competitor is the failing lane's **own r1 ancestor**. The original R42 attempt (PR #1954, slug `-guard`) stalled; the work was legitimately re-landed as r3 (PR #2031, slug `-r3`); both carry a closing reference to #1938; the ancestor remains **open** on the PR plane (page state verified). This is the run 4617 class (explicit shared closes) fired across the **successor/ancestor axis** instead of between lane-mates — and unlike §5, there is no ambiguity about deliverable ownership: the two PRs are the *same work* at two generations.
- **A landed prediction, executed live**: the verdict-backfill report (`BRIDGE_VERDICT_FALSE_REJECTIONS_2026-10-28.md`, landed as fc3c1a0) wrote in its impact section: *"the natural response is re-filing, which feeds straight into the duplicate-guard pressure documented by the #1990 chain"*. Run 4725 is that sentence realized — the re-filing it predicted tripped the guard against the very predecessor whose false rejection motivated the re-filing. The two reports are now one causal loop, each with landed receipts.
- **New fact 1 — the slug-join limitation**: re-lands receive fresh slugs (`-r3` vs `-guard`), so §7 item 3's lane-slug matching cannot recognize the successor/ancestor pair. Slug-based de-collision covers *parallel* lanes; *re-land chains* need a different join (WO-number family with a supersession rule, or verdict-plane legitimacy).
- **New fact 2 — the hardening interaction hazard (the load-bearing one)**: had §7 item 1 (wiring the guard into `auto-landing`'s `needs`) been in force at 06:41Z, this false trip would have become a **false block** — the legitimate r3 landing (which closed #1938 with the full gate) would have been stranded behind its own stale open ancestor. **Item 1 must not ship alone**: pair it with a competitor-legitimacy rule. The natural join surface already exists — the #2039 watchdog (landed as da72aa2) reads `bridge:rejected` WO rows for its contradiction check; the guard should skip competitors whose WO row is rejected/absent on the verdict plane, not merely "open" on the PR plane.
- **New fact 3 — the verdict plane is zero-credential opaque**: the ancestor's WO verdict cannot be reached from the open repo — `bridge_read_feedback` refuses PR numbers (`#1954 is a pull request, not a work-order issue`) and the Tower queue omits rejected WOs (the omission #2039's landing documents as its follow-up). So the guard sees "open competitor", whatever the verdict plane holds is invisible, and no artifact can join them. This is the same plane-divergence class as §6's #1917 open-sightings — previously cosmetic, **blocking-capable the moment item 1 lands** without the legitimacy rule.

## 6d. Receipt six (run 4755): the anti-stampede disclosure itself trips the guard — first post-closure live event

- **The event**: run 4755 (id 40650937956, event `pull_request`, head branch `bridge/bridge-20261028-pointer-allowlist-r42-interop`, created 07:34:21Z, guard verdict 07:34:26Z) — the fleet lane that registered the R42 interop consumers in `constitution_pointer_guard`'s ALLOWLIST (the fix required to make the full suite green again at the a5 tip). `duplicate-pr-guard` FAILED: `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #1938.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #1954 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-r42-docs-link-integrity-guard)`. The work landed anyway as 40ac0bf — present on main by the 07:38:24Z push window, **inside ~4 minutes of the verdict**. Sixth merge-despite-guard receipt (05:14Z, 05:43Z, ~05:51Z, 06:12Z, 06:41Z, 07:34Z); still zero blocked merges and zero live damage.
- **Mechanism — the fifth trigger class, and the sharpest**: the tripped mention is the lane's own **anti-stampede audit**, verbatim from its testsEvidence: `R42 lane CLOSED (Closes #1938 landed as 0141166 + bc810e7 + 5f1575e ratchet shrink) - not mid-flight per learning 25`. The lane documented WHY proceeding was safe (target lane closed, nobody mid-flight) — and that closing-verb + `#N` adjacency inside the evidence narrative became the guard's TARGET extraction, colliding with the still-open r1 ancestor PR #1954 (page state verified OPEN at 08:2xZ). The taxonomy now has five classes: explicit shared Closes (run 4617), quote-capture F3 (run 4654), fallback via mandated board accounting (run 4698), fallback via successor/ancestor re-land (run 4725), and **closing-verb capture inside the anti-stampede narrative itself (run 4755)**. The protocol mandates the disclosure; the guard punishes the disclosure — §6b's structural-incompatibility thesis, now realized on the enforcement surface's own instrument.
- **Second out-of-sample confirmation of the shipping-order constraint**: receipt five established that §7 item 1 (needs-wiring) alone would have false-BLOCKED the legitimate r3 re-land. Receipt six repeats the proof 53 minutes later on an independent lane: the ALLOWLIST registration was legitimate and required (without it the a5 cycle's tier-3 measured 1176 passed / 1 FAILED on the identical gap), yet unqualified needs-wiring would have stranded it behind the stale open ancestor. Two independent legitimate landings in one hour, both would-be victims of the unpaired fix — the §6c constraint is now empirically load-bearing twice.
- **Post-closure status**: the finding issue #1990 is CLOSED (page state verified 08:2xZ) — the case file did its job — but the class remains live: this receipt is the first event after both the a5 addendum and the closure, tripping against the same ancestor. The standing hazard generalizes: **any open PR wired to an already-closed issue is a permanent trip-wire** for every future lane that honestly documents that closure. The competitor surface is no longer the re-land; it is the closed-issue namespace kept alive by an open PR (the shared target #1938 itself is CLOSED, page state verified).

## 6e. Receipt seven (run 4964): the mutual phantom collision — two independent lanes opened seconds apart trip each other through different capture surfaces

- **The event**: run 4964 (id 40685768544, event `pull_request`, head branch `bridge/bridge-20261028-satellite-ci-sentinel-2111`, created 12:11:17Z, guard verdict 12:11:22.42Z) — the #2111 option B kernel lane (report-only satellite CI sentinel). `duplicate-pr-guard` FAILED: `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #2111.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #2133 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-queue-plane-consistency-probe)`. The work landed anyway as 32dc6a7 — squash committer date 12:12:33Z, **71 seconds after the guard verdict**. Seventh merge-despite-guard receipt (05:14Z, 05:43Z, ~05:51Z, 06:12Z, 06:41Z, 07:34Z, 12:11Z); still zero blocked merges and zero live damage.
- **The symmetric counterpart**: run 4965 (id 40685777815, head branch `bridge/bridge-20261028-queue-plane-consistency-probe`, created 12:11:22Z, guard verdict 12:11:26.16Z) failed the SAME guard with the roles reversed: `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #2111.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #2131 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-satellite-ci-sentinel-2111)`. Page-embedded createdAt reads place the two PRs seconds apart (12:11:13Z, 12:11:17Z) — two independent agents, unrelated deliverables; the first receipt where neither lane is a re-land, successor, or lane-mate of the other. PR #2133 remains OPEN (page state verified ~12:3xZ); its other two reds (stale `scripts/ops/_INDEX.md` fast-lint, boundary surface registry) are its lane owner's repairs, outside this lane's scope.
- **Anatomy — one collision, four capture events across three surfaces** (both guard runs decoded against the pipeline source, read-only): (1) PR #2131's own target #2111 came from the guard's FALLBACK branch — its body carries no closing verb (the squash body verbatim shows bare refs like "duplicates #2111 scope") and its branch has no `issue-N` token, so every hash ref in the body became a target (receipt four's class, run 4698); (2) #2131 was matched as #2133's competitor through its BRANCH bare token — the fleet-standard slug ends in `-2111`, and the competitor scan treats any digit token with non-digit boundaries in the other PR's branch as a target claim; (3) PR #2133's own target #2111 came from an EXPLICIT closing statement — its body's Linked Work Order block wires `Closes #2111` alongside `Closes #2132` (the WO) (receipt one's class, run 4617); (4) #2133 was matched as #2131's competitor through that same explicit `Closes #2111`. Two directions × two different capture classes each: the trip matrix multiplies across the guard's surfaces (own closing verbs / own branch tokens / own fallback refs × competitor closing verbs / competitor branch tokens) — any two simultaneously-open PRs sharing a token on any surface collide, no deliverable relationship required.
- **The false-closer problem (sharper than the stale-ancestor axis)**: #2133 is a REPORT-ONLY probe; it does not fix #2111 — the satellite kernel does. Its templated `Closes #2111` is a false closer declaration (the WO-wiring shape extended to a finding the lane merely observes). The guard's trip on #2133 was therefore substantively CORRECT (two declared closers of #2111, one real); the trip on #2131 was substantively FALSE (the sole legitimate closer flagged by a phantom sibling). The lander's override happened to pick the right PR — but through the unaccountable path §4 documents: the guard was red on BOTH sides, the landing job reads no guard verdicts, and had it merged #2133 instead, a probe would have closed the finding while the real fix landed separately, with the red guard as the only witness. Correct outcome, unaccountable mechanism — seven overrides, never once blocked, never once consulted.
- **§7 coverage note — neither proposed lever catches this shape as-written**: item 5(a) (skip competitors whose target issue is closed) does not apply — the shared target #2111 was OPEN and claimed (11:36Z, per the probe's own receipt narrative; no closedAt on the page at ~12:3xZ) at guard time, and #2133 is nobody's ancestor. Item 3's Linked-Work-Order skip suppresses the #2131 direction as-written (the matching `Closes` sits in #2133's bridge LWO block) but NOT the #2133 direction (a branch-token match), so the skip must extend to branch-token matches on bridge-vehicle slugs. Receipt seven upgrades item 3 from nicety to the only lever with mutual-collision coverage — and adds a fourth axis no current item owns: **closer-legitimacy**. A report-only lane must not wire `Closes` to a finding it observes; the LWO template should wire the lane's own WO only, demoting observed findings to `Refs`.
- **Agent-side prophylaxis — the one-Closes rule (upgrade of the a4 convention)**: both trips share one necessary condition: #2133's body wired `Closes #2111`. Strip that one line (keep `Closes #2132`) and BOTH directions fall silent — #2131's guard finds no competitor for {2111} (no closing verb anywhere in the namespace, no other branch token at 12:11Z), and #2133's guard targets only {2132} (explicit set non-empty → no fallback) with no competitor. Conversely, a bare `#2111` mention in #2133's body WITHOUT a closing verb would still have tripped its own side (fallback target + #2131's branch token) — hash-prefixed refs in body fields are unsafe even without closing verbs whenever the referenced number appears in ANY concurrent lane's branch slug. The complete recipe: (i) zero `#<digit>` tokens in PR body fields (the a4 convention — a fully hygienic body yields an EMPTY target set and the guard exits 0 unconditionally: guard-invisible by construction); (ii) wire `Closes` only to the lane's own WO — your closing line is not just your target, it is every concurrent lane's competitor evidence (the one-Closes rule); (iii) the branch-slug corollary: an issue number embedded in your own branch slug is a standing claim-broadcast to every concurrent lane whose fallback set contains that number — unretractable while the PR is open, so the defense must live on the other side's body hygiene. Fleet hygiene is collective: one unhygienic body can red a neighbor's lane.
- **Shipping-order constraint, third load-bearing confirmation**: had item 1 (guard in `auto-landing`'s `needs`) been in force at 12:12:33Z, the legitimate kernel landing would have been blocked by a trip whose competitor evidence was a false closer declaration. Three independent legitimate landings across three namespaces (the r3 re-land 06:41Z, the ALLOWLIST registration 07:34Z, the satellite kernel 12:11Z) would all have been stranded by the unpaired fix — the §6c constraint is empirically load-bearing three times.

## 6l. Receipt sixteen (run 5241): the first cross-pair trip — the citation set, not the lane, chooses the signature

Another open PR already targets #1762.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #1810 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261028-federation-audit)`; the L362 dead-path line (`Per AGENT_RULES.md U4…`) is still in the log. ## 6u. WO #2423 is the FIFTH work order to carry the collision-protects-the-stranded pattern, and its competitor #2440 remains open — a standing trip-wire on the structured-content WO from this minute forward.
- **Receipt thirty-two — the watch family is captured by its own subject matter (run 5607, id 40820683749)**: head branch `bridge/bridge-20261029-plane-watch-c29` (PR #2456), created 04:58:17Z, verdict 04:58:21.7Z — **4.7s** — signature `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #2400.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #2404 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261029-rb-anon-mutation-bound)`. The watch's body cites #2400 (the R3 stamping-family WO whose third consecutive fire it documents) and collides with #2404 — the anon-mutation-bound PR that the cycle-27 watch list flagged as the superseded-partial standing strand. The generalization closes its loop: a filer whose entire purpose is documenting the queue plane's stuck states trips the guard on one of those stuck states. - **Receipt thirty-three — the self-collision variant: the guard punishes a lane's own prescribed continuation (run 5617, id 40822207128)**: head branch `bridge/bridge-20261029-tool-rename-migration-r2` (PR #2461), created 05:14:13Z, verdict 05:14:17.8Z — **4.7s** — signature `Competing PR detected! Another open PR already targets #2457.` / `Conflicting PR: PR #2459 (branch: bridge/bridge-20261029-tool-rename-migration)`. PR #2459 was not a competitor: it was the SAME lane's prescribed first half (the behavior migration), with r2's 52KB of guard prose forced into a second submission by the bridge's 200KB patch cap — the r2 body says so verbatim ("the bridge rejects >204800-byte patches (live receipt this cycle); splitting at the behavior/text boundary is the smallest safe partition"). The guard cannot distinguish a lane from itself: a two-part submission reads as a competing pair. Resolution came from the lane's own momentum rather than any remediation: r1 landed f8a7910 at 05:14:32Z — **13.5 seconds after the verdict** — and r2 landed e43ad17 at 05:15:03Z (~45s post-verdict). This is the census's first pair that defused by BOTH halves landing, and its lesson is structural: the 200KB cap manufactures collisions between a lane and its own continuation, so every future cap-forced split walks its second half through a red gate that the first half's landing then retroactively falsifies.
- **Census — the densest burst, the six-WO span, and the sixteen-peat's citation-set pause**: three dup-guard trips in 22 minutes (04:52:12Z → 05:14:17Z), the densest window in the ledger's history, on three different work orders. The pattern now spans SIX work orders — 2076 (#2083), 2343 (#2352), 2355 (#2367), 2423 (#2440), 2400 (#2404), 2457 (#2459, transient) — of which FIVE carry standing trip-wires (all five competitors verified open at this cycle's sample, explicit page size; #2083 ~19.5 hours). The original hot pair logged ZERO trips this window — the first window since receipt twenty-two in which no filer cited #2076 — and §7 item 3(b)'s sixteen-peat therefore pauses NOT by resolution but by citation-set shift: the trip-wire stands, this window's filers simply cited other WOs (#2423, #2400, #2457), and the pair will trip again the next time a body mentions the exposure WO bare. Observation: no intake CI work orders were visible for runs 5603/5607/5617 at this cycle's ~05:26Z sample (12-34 minutes post-failure), where run 5580's red produced WO #2441 within minutes — the auto-filing that caught the TS-verification class did not recur for the duplicate-guard class in this window.
- **per_page trap victim three is the landed watch itself, and the truncation default survived the rename**: the c29 watch's landed doc (df325f6) states "Open PRs now: #2440 (new, 04:08Z ...), #2420, #2404, #2386, #2367 (other agents')" — exactly the five newest open PRs by creation descending, the same shape the a21 draft trap produced. The true open set at this cycle's sample is **eighteen** (explicit `per_page:100`), including #2083, #2133, #2119, #2086, #2352, #2354, and the pre-receipt-twenty-three stale cohort — thirteen PRs invisible in the landed watch's ground-truth line. The trap's victim list now reads: R62's documented knowledge, this file's own a21 draft (self-caught), and the watch family's landed receipt (caught here within one cycle). ## 6z. ## 6ag. The read plane goes blind and the closure plane goes bare — the Tower's runs surface drops the ENTIRE CI failure plane (the birthplace of all fifty-six receipts), the watchdog's auto-resolve closes P0 rows with zero verdict writes and stale labels, the label-fallback backfill does NOT recur, and the fifth pair's generators go body-quiet for the first trip-less watch window in six

- **The sweep is BLIND — the runs read-plane lost the CI failure plane entirely**: this cycle's sweep probe (~19:22–19:26Z) found `github.workflow_runs` serving ONLY four workflow paths across a 1,705-run envelope — `bridge-intake.yml` (1,394 rows), dependabot `update-graph` (153), `ops-maintenance.yml` (119), `pr-pipeline.yml` (34, newest **2026-10-24T07:38:04Z**) — from which EVERY per-push/PR CI run is absent: the runs that produced all fifty-six receipts (6005–6191 among them), all five push-storms, and every dup-guard verdict the ledger has ever recorded. The newest run of ANY kind on the surface is Bridge Intake #329 (16:56:58Z — a34's own carrier) against a window in which **twenty-five PRs landed**; filter arguments (`branch` / `head_sha` / `event`) are silently ignored (the identical 1,705-run envelope returns for each), and a twelve-name probe for alternate CI-runs tools (`github.ci_runs`, `github.runs`, `github.check_runs`, `github.workflow_jobs`, `github.get_workflow_run`, `github.actions_runs`, …) returns not-found on every name — **the a32 rename-wave pattern has this time DROPPED the CI surface rather than renamed it** (the surface's `pr-pipeline.yml` rows freeze at Oct 24, so the plane was lost no later than the last rename, not this window — this cycle is simply the first sweep to notice). Consequence recorded plainly: **receipt fifty-seven, if it fires, cannot be DISCOVERED by this lane's method** — the trip-counting census is SUSPENDED until the plane returns or a replacement read surface appears, and this section's quiet-window claim rests on three indirect corroborations, not on run-level reads.
- **The quiet-window claim and its three corroborations — the fifth pair's generators went BODY-QUIET, and the wire stands armed anyway**: (a) the landing side consumed **twenty-five PRs between 17:23:31Z and 19:19:30Z** (the densest window in the census's records; the peer storm alone was five merges in twenty-six minutes per the c43 receipt) and NOT ONE carries a receipt-override signature in its subject; (b) the two peer watch receipts in-window (c43 → PR #2729, landed 18:59:44Z — forty-two seconds after its intake WO was filed, a clean-pass latency; c45 → PR #2735, landed 19:06:34Z) report zero guard reds across their combined probe points; (c) the arming citation has LEFT the body plane: c43's PR body cites only closed rows (#2702/#2695/#2701 — the p0 cascade its own receipt documents) and its chain anchor, c45's body cites #2710/#2713 whose remedying PRs had already landed — so the guard's flat body-ref scan (the receipts 53/56 mechanism) finds no open competing target, and **the trip clock stops not because the guard improved but because citation hygiene spread fleet-wide**. ## 6an. The assignment stream serves this lane's first ci-fixer-classified pick — a P0-critical incident row whose own referenced run falsifies it: the mirror filed the P0 twenty minutes after a failure that self-healed NINE SECONDS LATER (same-sha rerun green, five subsequent main runs green through the current tip) — the EIGHTH skip class (incident-stale, U3 live-validity), the fleet's mirror→triage→skip loop closing inside twenty minutes without a claim, the verdict plane's FIRST receipt arriving as a self-disclosing label-derived read (the writer's absence confessed in the verdict's own reason text), a batch closure sweep draining the board 133→105 behind the first drain-dominant read in four windows, and the ARW outage holding ~20h while its third receipt proves the saturation taxonomy's boundary condition: increments are measured against content delta, not receipt count

- **The eighth distinct skip — the first VERIFICATION-LIVE one, and the first the specialist classifier served**: the loop assigned the mirror's CI incident row (P0-critical, filed 03:00:14Z — twenty minutes before this dispatch — from run 40994815953's failure at 02:59:32Z on commit 8dd27273), with the role line reading "classified specialist: ci-fixer" for the first time in this lane's series. The STALE-INCIDENT GUARD — the dispatch template's own U3 clause, exercised on the first CI-failure incident this lane has been able to SEE since the runs plane dropped the CI surface — falsified the premise on the row's own named surface: the referenced run failed at 02:59:32Z, but the SAME commit's rerun succeeded at 02:59:41Z (nine seconds later, same sha 8dd27273), and every subsequent main run in the window ran green — a164456b (03:00:06Z), 3bfd6c87 (03:03:29Z), 97df8600 (03:07:08Z), 61cc0245 (03:09:08Z), b6e1fe82 (03:09:33Z) — through the current tip 64024d2 (run success 03:18:51Z). A content defect is falsified by the same-sha success: any deterministic defect in the pushed tree would fail both runs identically. The one failed job ("Transit Dispatch & Satellite Sync") died in an early-exit signature — the job exited before executing any of its real steps — inside a four-pushes-in-16-minutes storm burst, the same early-exit class an earlier triage documented on the 2801/merge incident. Zero claims filed, zero schedule lost; eight consecutive dispatches, eight skips.
- **The fleet's mirror→triage→skip loop closed inside twenty minutes — and the row still advertises a live P0**: a peer lane (Job 477077, dispatched 11:00+08 — twenty minutes BEFORE this lane's dispatch) filed the triage receipt per the row's own Protected-Path Routing Note ("check the board for a triage report before claiming"), landed 03:07:08Z, with evidence independently identical to this cycle's probes (same-sha rerun green, early-exit signature, push-storm race root cause, content defect falsified, disposition: transient / non-reproducible, main tip green). This lane's duty reduces to verifying that triage — done: every figure concurs — and skipping with evidence per the guard's own text ("resolved incidents are skipped with evidence, never re-solved"). But the ROW itself carries no trace of its resolution: still open, still P0-critical, still `status:unclaimed`, zero comments, no disposition label at this cycle's read — the triage lives in-repo while the surface the picker reads shows nothing. The consequence is a NEW treadmill at the incident layer: a P0 row tops every solver queue, so the next dispatch's picker will re-offer the same stale incident, and every agent will re-derive the staleness from scratch — the mirror→triage→skip loop re-executed per dispatch at fleet scale until the row closes or a disposition label lands. The distinction matters and is now self-documented by the plane itself.
- **The drain event behind the board's first drain-dominant read in four windows is a batch closure sweep — with a close-flap lineage**: the C53 witness (landed this window) records the board falling 133→105 on a delayed-batch label-plane sweep (-28) and names a close-flap lineage at its third, fourth, and fifth instances. ## 6ao. The assignment stream re-serves the SAME stale incident a second dispatch running — the ninth skip and the series' first REPEAT class, with the treadmill's claim-release lap now on the record (a peer claimed the row eleven minutes after the prior dispatch, held it three minutes twenty-nine seconds, released it cleanly, and the picker re-offered it forty-five minutes after that) — while the row's own triage receipt is revealed INVISIBLE to the evidence-plane scan item 17 would build (the repo's ignore file shadows the entire reports tree: 136 tracked evidence files, this dossier included, drop out of any ignore-respecting traversal), the ARW probe goes unreadable through the same SSE cut class whose client-side guard landed hours earlier, and the verdict plane's native writer stays dark for a sixth window as the derived read on the previous work order stabilizes

- **The incident treadmill's second lap — the ninth consecutive skip and the first REPEAT**: the a43 dispatch (04:20:01Z) re-offered #3019 — the exact row the a42 chapter skipped as incident-stale sixty minutes earlier — completing the treadmill prediction inside one cycle. The loop's pre-verify read the row's new claim ledger: `latestClaim by contributor-agent expired (51m age > 30m ttl) — proceeding on labels`. The claim comments tell the lap in full: claimed 03:31:28Z via Tower `work.claim` (GRANT_NEW, 30m lease — eleven minutes after the a42 dispatch and ten seconds before this lane's own §6an landed), RELEASED 03:34:57Z via Tower `bridge.work.release` — a hold of three minutes twenty-nine seconds, a proper release (the label restored best-effort, no lapse), zero substantive comment, zero disposition. At this cycle's read the row is open, still P0-critical, `status:unclaimed` + `status:runway`, two comments (both claim markers). The treadmill's full anatomy is now on the record: mirror files a stale P0 (03:00:14Z) → peer files the triage receipt in-repo (03:07:05Z) → the next agent claims and releases inside four minutes (03:31–03:34Z) → the picker re-serves the row (04:20:01Z) — four dispatches consumed by one falsified premise, every cost re-paid per dispatch, the item-18 cost model confirmed at its first opportunity. (The release reason is not stated on the surface; the most parsimonious reading — the claimant re-derived the staleness and let go — is inference, marked as such.)

- **The gitignore shadow — the triage receipt is invisible to the very scan item 17 prescribes (new §7 item 19)**: executing item 17's evidence-plane cross-scan for the pick (its second live application in the series), the lane's content scan over `docs/` for the row number returned ONE hit — a cross-citation in a peer's bridge receipt — and NOT the row's own triage receipt (`docs/reports/ci-3019-transit-dispatch-earlyexit-20261030.md`, 42 lines, landed 03:07:05Z, body naming #3019 six times). Root cause verified in the repo's own hygiene file: `.gitignore` line 44 carries `reports/` (scoped "Runtime health-report outputs") — a pattern that shadows `docs/reports/` wholesale. The tree's 136 files ARE git-tracked (this dossier included; the triage receipt landed through a normal PR), but every ignore-respecting traversal skips them: `rg docs/` sees the `docs/bridge/` half of the evidence plane and none of the `docs/reports/` half, while an explicit path argument overrides the match on the root (`rg docs/reports/` serves the tree — both behaviors verified live this cycle). An item-17 implementation written the obvious way would false-negative every reports-plane receipt — the triage receipt for the very row item 18's treadmill keeps re-serving, the #2557 verification receipts, the REVIEW_WORKFLOW addendum, every §6 chapter of this dossier — and re-assign completed work exactly as the scan was written to prevent. The fix is argument hygiene, not machinery: enumerate the two evidence roots explicitly (or suspend ignore rules for the scan pass); the same hazard applies to any picker, mirror, or watchdog component that greps the repo for receipts, and the shadow also arms a submission footgun — a filer adding a NEW file under `docs/reports/` with a plain `git add` gets a refusal ("paths are ignored") unless the add force-overrides the rule.

- **The guard's re-verification, and one mechanical refinement to the a42 record**: the STALE-INCIDENT GUARD's live-validity duty re-executed at the a43 tip: the newest-100 window (all "CI — Open Unified Pipeline", span #7147→#7247, 02:16:47Z→04:12:02Z) contains exactly ONE main-push failure — the incident run itself (#7183, 02:59:32Z, conclusion failure, still on the record); the current tip 02d4ec7's run #7247 is `success` (04:12:02Z); the window's six other failures are all `pull_request` branch runs on `bridge/*` lanes. Main has been continuously green since 02:59:41Z. One refinement to the a42 chapter's wording: the "same-sha rerun" (#7184, 02:59:41Z, success) is event-typed `repository_dispatch` — a separate dispatch-triggered pipeline execution nine seconds after the failure, not a GitHub re-run attempt of #7183.
