## 7. Proposed admin-side hardening (NOT agent-actionable; protected path)

Ordered by leverage:

1. **Wire the guard into the landing gate**: add `duplicate-pr-guard` to `auto-landing`'s `needs`. One line; makes U4 enforcement real (a guard failure then trips `!contains(needs.*.result, 'failure')` and blocks the merge). This is the single change that closes the disconnect observed in run 4617.
2. **Forensic metadata in the guard error**: include the competitor's `state` and `updated_at` (already present in the `gh pr list --json` payload) in the `::error` lines, so the next sighting-vs-ground-truth divergence is self-documenting.
3. **De-collide shared-WO closers**: either require the competitor to match the WO's own lane slug token (the `bridge-YYYYMMDD-slug` join key the landing watchdog already standardizes) instead of body closing refs, or skip competitors whose matching `Closes` sits in a bridge Linked Work Order block of a satellite-vehicle PR. Goal: a fixer PR should never be flagged by its own lane's vehicle.
4. **(Optional) unify the verdict planes**: run the guard on push events too, or make push-event bridge runs reuse the PR run's results. Lower priority — after (1), the guard-free push plane cannot reach the merge path, so it degrades to harmless redundancy.
5. **Stale-ancestor hygiene (from §6d)**: the competitor the guard flags may be an open PR whose shared target issue is already closed — definitionally superseded work (receipts five AND six both tripped against the open r1 ancestor PR #1954 while its shared target #1938 was CLOSED on the issue plane). Two composable skips: (a) one extra issue-state read in the guard's existing `gh` surface — skip competitors whose target issue is closed; (b) admin-side closing of superseded re-land ancestors (PR #1954 at this writing) removes the live trip-wire immediately. This composes with the §6c verdict-plane legitimacy rule into a two-axis competitor-legitimacy check: PR-plane open + target-issue closed → stale; PR-plane open + WO verdict rejected/absent → illegitimate. Neither rule alone covers both axes observed.
6. **Content-hash collision check (from §6z)**: the guard's body-citation scan missed the triple landing's TRUE collision — three byte-identical submissions of the same receipt to the same path three minutes apart (receipts forty-four/forty-five/forty-six) — because it detects only hash-ref citations, not identical artifacts. A submission-side (or gate-side) content-hash check — same `scopePaths` + same file hash already on an open PR or freshly landed → fail closed with the duplicate PR numbers in the error — would have caught exactly the one trip class in forty-seven receipts where overriding caused actual harm, and it composes with item 1 (a wired content-collision gate blocks the re-lands instead of merely documenting them).
7. **Batch-chain awareness (from §6ab)**: receipt fifty's collision is manufactured by the fleet's own batch-slicing convention — one parent work order, sequential batch PRs (1a/1b), each body citing the parent WO bare for task tracking; the guard's citation scan reads the siblings as competitors. A competitor-legitimacy refinement — skip competitors whose branch shares the filer's lane-slug base with a differing batch marker (the same `bridge-YYYYMMDD-slug` join key item 3 already standardizes, extended with the `bNa/bNb` batch suffix), or whose shared citation resolves to the filer's own parent task — would have suppressed exactly this class while leaving true-duplicate detection intact. It composes with item 3 (lane-slug join key) and item 6 (content-hash): the batch case has NEITHER identical content NOR a shared path, so only intent-aware suppression covers it — and receipt fifty is the proof that the override pattern already handles the class harmlessly, so the refinement's value is noise reduction at the gate, not harm prevention.
8. **Submission-side backoff / branch-level run-rate cap (from §6ad)**: §6ad's push-storm shows a submission client retrying without backoff can hit the pipeline thirty-one times in sixty-six seconds on one branch (runs 6048–6080, 27 concurrency-cancelled by their own successors) — burning run numbers, queue slots, and Actions minutes at a rate the landing plane cannot consume, and (in the worst counterfactual) doing so while a guard trip class is armed, which would multiply reds faster than any override discipline could audit them. Two composable caps: (a) client-side — the submit helper should treat a fresh run appearing for the same head SHA as success-of-dispatch and enter exponential backoff (the lane's own submit pattern already forbids same-taskId reruns; extend that to push-level); (b) workflow-side — a per-branch concurrency group keyed on the head branch (already partially present via the cancel-in-progress behavior the storm itself demonstrated) plus a minimum interval between push-triggered runs of the same branch. Neither touches the guard's detection logic; both shrink the noise floor the guard's ledger has to be read through.
9. **Citation-token boundary validation (from §6ae)**: receipt fifty-five's target was minted from the filer's own TITLE token `addendum#2` — a label whose `#2` fragment the flat citation scan reads as an issue reference (the landed commit subject carries the token byte-exact). A minimal token-validity rule — a `#<digits>` match only counts as a citation when it is not immediately preceded by a word character (regex word-boundary before `#`), optionally composed with item 2's forensic metadata (log the matched line so the next label-vs-reference divergence is self-documenting) — would have left this PR green without touching any true positive in the ledger: every receipt's verbatim signature (`targets #NNNN`) came from prose or WO-block references, never from an attached label. This composes with items 3 and 5 (competitor-legitimacy axes) but is strictly cheaper: it is a single character-class check on the filer's own scan, admin-side, with zero additional reads.
10. **Backfill observability (from §6af)**: the drain's first movement in ledger history arrived as a batch sweep (thirteen rows closed newest-first in ~3m09s) executed inside a scheduled intake run whose conclusion is `failure` while its job-level surface exposes ZERO failed jobs, and which silently skipped its youngest queued row (#2677, age ~48s at sweep start) with no marker or stated criterion. A drain this consequential must be legible: (a) the sweep step should emit its batch record — row list, per-row outcome (closed/rejected/skipped), and the skip reason for every ineligible row; (b) the intake run's failure should attribute to a named job (composing with WO #2673's boundary-emptiness assertions: a run-level conclusion with an empty failed-job set is itself an assertable anomaly); (c) the derived-verdict display should link the sweep run that produced it, so a future auditor can go from `APPLIED_LABEL_DERIVED` on a row to the exact batch and run id in one hop. This composes with item 2 (forensic metadata) but targets the drain plane rather than the guard plane; its cost is one structured log line per row the sweep already touches.
11. **Closure-plane label reconciliation (from §6ag)**: the watchdog's auto-resolve now closes mirror-marker issues via a direct GitHub PATCH with `state_reason: completed` while touching NOTHING else — the observed result is closed rows that still carry their dispatch-time labels (`status:claimed` on #2692, `status:unclaimed` on #2695/#2225, plus `handoff:coder` and `P0-critical`) and empty verdict planes. A closed-but-still-claimed row is a false claim-slot that the anti-stampede readers may honor for the claim's TTL, and a closed-but-still-unclaimed P0 misstates the queue to any label-driven census. The reconcile is one PATCH-shaped follow-up per closure, in the same function that already writes the auto-resolution comment: strip or annotate the `status:*` labels at close time (and, if the verdict plane is deliberately write-less for anonymous-bridge rows, say so in the closure comment the way the label-fallback sweep's derived verdict does — the two closure paths disagree on disclosure style today: one explains itself, the other is silent). This composes with item 10 (drain observability — same plane, same ask for legibility) and item 2 (forensic metadata); it does not ask the watchdog to write verdicts, only to leave labels consistent with the state it just changed.
12. **Guard verdict self-attestation (from §6ah)**: the duplicate-pr-guard's verdict currently has exactly one durable record — the run conclusion on the CI plane — and §6ag/§6ah show that plane can drop without notice, taking fifty-six receipts' worth of trip telemetry with it and leaving a re-armed trip-wire's firing permanently unanswerable (a tripped PR lands via override regardless, so the merge record cannot substitute). The guard already holds everything needed for a self-attesting write: at the END of every execution, pass or fail, it should append one structured line — run id, head SHA, competitor set scanned, citations matched, verdict — to a committed ledger artifact (or, failing that, a deterministic PR comment keyed to the head SHA). Cost: one file append per guard execution. Value: the trip census survives any read-plane collapse (the ledger doc can then reconstruct trips from the repo tree alone, the way it already reconstructs landings from merge commits); a guard silenced by infrastructure becomes DISTINGUISHABLE from a guard that never ran; and the §6ah natural experiment — did the re-armed body trip? — stops depending on the survival of an unrelated workflow surface. This composes with item 1 (a wired guard needs its verdicts auditable to be enforceable) and item 2 (forensic metadata): the attestation line IS the forensic metadata, written by the only component that knows it.
13. **Resolution-contract-aware candidate picking (from §6ai)**: the loop's candidate picker reads only labels and claim state, and three consecutive dispatches caught it misreading a DIFFERENT axis each time — a35's row was premise-dead (its named surfaces did not exist in-repo), a36's was claim-stale (a live TTL claim under an `status:unclaimed` label), and a37's row was contract-forbidden: the challenge's own body declared "**report-only; requests reopen OR explicit verdict; NOT auto-closure**" with the resolution authority explicitly admin-side, while the picker's assignment template instructed a Closes-reference PR — whose merge auto-closure would have repeated the exact violation the challenge protests. The stale-claim half of this gap is already fixed (the loop's pick path now re-verifies live claims before emitting a candidate); the missing half is one body scan in the same pick path: a row whose body declares a resolution contract (report-only / challenge / NOT-auto-closure / requests-reopen / admin-HITL-required markers) should be excluded from the Closes-PR template or re-scoped to an evidence-adjudication instruction, the way the spec-kit assessment's kill criteria re-scoped a35's row. Cost: one marker-list check per candidate at pick time, before any agent burns a dispatch on a structurally unclaimable row. This composes with item 2 (forensic metadata — the marker list is machine-readable the moment it is written down) and with the automation precedent the stale-claim preverify landing already set.
14. **Remedy-location-aware candidate picking (from §6aj)**: the fourth picker axis is SPATIAL, and neither the labels nor the body's contract markers expose it — the a38 pick was claimable (the pre-verify aged out the stale claim) and premise-live (the named services verifiably still broken), and the skip rested entirely on where the fix must land: the row's own Recovery section said "requires admin", the root cause lived in a satellite repo with no PR-submission path from the open repo's Single Door, and the deploy re-trigger is a service-redeploy — all three remedy classes NAMED in the dispatch template's own carve-out, yet the picker offered the row to a solver anyway. The row's own history shows the cost: SIX claim cycles in 28 hours, five released within minutes, zero landings — a claim-and-release treadmill manufactured by a P1 label pointing at an out-of-envelope remedy. The missing half-check is one location scan in the same pick path that already re-verifies claims: extract the row's remedy markers (requires-admin / needs-write-access / satellite repo names / service or console verbs in Recovery and Escalation sections) and, when every named remedy resolves outside the open repo, emit the evidence-submission instruction INSTEAD of the solver assignment — the row still gets its evidence receipt (the peer evidence-refresh pattern proves the value), but no agent burns a claim cycle discovering the boundary by claiming and releasing. Cost: one marker scan per candidate at pick time, reusing the same body-parse the contract-marker check of item 13 needs. This composes with item 13 (shared body-scan pass, two marker lists), with item 11 (the released-claim treadmill also leaves stale `status:*` labels for the next reader), and with the boundary-honest template fix that landed this window (the carve-out sentence tells the AGENT where the line is; this item teaches the PICKER to read it first).
15. **Landing-receipt cross-scan candidate picking (from §6ak)**: the fifth picker axis is TEMPORAL-DELIVERY, and this one hides behind the picker working PERFECTLY — the a39 pick was claimable (the pre-verify aged out the 2,484-minute-stale claim), premise-live (the named gap verifiably still exists at hub level), contract-clean (no report-only or NOT-auto-closure marker), and fully in-envelope (the in-repo slice is exactly what the row promises), and the skip rested on ONE fact no label, claim state, or contract marker exposes: the row's own body had already been OBEYED — its single promised in-envelope artifact ("Full dossier lands in-repo as `docs/...` (companion PR)") landed on main 1h33m before the dispatch, 47h16m32s after filing. The picker reads the body to extract a TASK; it never checks whether the body's PROMISE is already kept — and a row whose in-envelope half is delivered leaves only the out-of-envelope half, so every solver assignment it emits is a duplicate-minting invitation (U4's founding class, caught pre-claim here only because this lane re-scans the tree before claiming). The missing half-check is one existence scan in the same pick path that already re-verifies claims and parses bodies: extract the row's promised-artifact markers (in-repo paths, "companion PR", "lands in-repo as", "dossier at", "full dossier in") and test each against the current tree; when every promised in-envelope artifact EXISTS, emit the evidence-submission instruction instead of the solver assignment — the row still gets its confirmation receipt without minting a duplicate landing. Cost: one path-existence check per candidate at pick time, reusing the same body-parse items 13 and 14 need (three marker lists, one scan pass). This composes with item 6 (content-hash collision check — the submission-side twin of the same gap: the picker's scan keeps solvers OFF delivered rows, the guard's hash check keeps duplicate PRs OFF the queue; together they cover the duplicate lifecycle at both ends) and with item 11 (the row that triggered this item still rides `status:unclaimed` with an empty verdict plane — nothing on the label or verdict surface distinguishes a delivered row from an open one, so the body-promise scan is the only pick-time signal available).
16. **Challenge-aware closure gating (from §6al)**: the ledger has now watched a challenge WIN and its subject RE-CLOSED 67 minutes later — the closure paths (the watchdog's auto-resolve, the on-merge auto-close, an agent's Closes-merge) are state-blind (they misread the state they change), verdict-blind (they write nothing to the verdict surface), and now demonstrated CHALLENGE-blind: none of them reads whether the row it is closing is the named subject of an open or recently-granted challenge. The #2225 loop is the cost model: closed 19:09Z → challenge filed 19:11Z → reopened 22:11:26Z (challenge granted) → re-closed 23:18:17Z (zero verdict, claim-close signature 3m19s after an agent claim) → re-challenged 00:09:12Z — one challenge consumed per lap, each lap eroding the tracking board's trust exactly as Challenge #2's own text argues. The gate is one read per closure in every automated closer: before writing `state=closed`, check the row's recent history for a challenge-granted reopen and the open row set for an `audit:finding` challenge naming this row as subject; on a hit, block the closure and emit the verdict-plane instruction instead — the challenge row already carries the enforced closure criterion the closer should be honoring, so the gate routes the closer to the criterion rather than past it. Cost: one history read per closure, the same read item 11's label reconciliation already needs at close time. This composes with item 11 (legible labels), item 12 (written verdicts), and item 10 (drain observability) — together the four make an automated closure legible, accountable, and contestable; without all four the board runs a close/reopen treadmill that burns one witness cycle per lap.
17. **Evidence-plane cross-scan candidate picking (from §6am)**: the sixth picker axis is COMMUNITY-DELIVERY, and it hides behind item 15 working PERFECTLY — the a41 pick was claimable (the pre-verify aged out the 39-minute-stale claim), premise-live (both tool probes returned the row's byte-identical error ~14.5 hours after filing), contract-clean (no resolution-contract markers; the body proposes concrete fixes), and item-15-clean in the only sense that scan can check: the body promises NO in-repo artifact at all, so a promised-artifact existence scan passes vacuously — every named remedy resolves operator-plane (a Render service env var, a backend deploy confirmation that the peer verification receipt shows is a PRECONDITION, a tower-runtime redeploy; the receipt's two-layer finding means even the env-var half would trade the config error for a 404 against the deployed backends). The skip rested on ONE fact no label, claim state, contract marker, or body-promise scan exposes: the row's agent-side lifecycle was already COMPLETE — the dispatch template's prescribed substitute for out-of-envelope rows ("submit evidence via the bridge and do NOT auto-close") had been executed TWICE (the Oct-29 two-layer verification receipt localizing the outage to a named render service and a 43-key env listing; the c34 addendum landed 37 minutes before the dispatch filing the byte-identical re-verification), so the only deliverable a solver could produce is a duplicate of an existing receipt — the U4 founding class wearing an evidence costume, and the third row in four dispatches whose assignment was minted for work that no longer existed or never could exist. The missing half-check is one reference scan in the same pick path items 13-15 already need: extract the row number and scan the landed evidence plane (the docs/bridge/ and docs/reports/ trees — filenames first, contents on filename hits) for receipts naming it; when the evidence plane already serves the row AND the row's remedy markers resolve outside the open repo (item 14's marker list), emit a residue classification (or nothing) instead of a solver assignment — the row's evidence need is served, its closure belongs to the admin lane, and no agent should burn a dispatch discovering that by claiming and releasing. Cost: one filename grep per candidate at pick time, reusing the same body-parse pass items 13-15 need (four marker lists, one scan pass). This composes with item 15 (its temporal twin: item 15 keeps solvers off rows whose OWN promises are kept, item 17 keeps solvers off rows whose COMMUNITY receipts are kept — together they cover both ways a row's agent-side work can already be done), with item 14 (the remedy-location markers that gate when the evidence-plane check applies), and with item 6 (the submission-side content-hash check — the board-side twin of the same duplicate lifecycle: picker-side scans keep solvers OFF completed rows, the guard keeps duplicate PRs OFF the queue).
18. **Transient-failure gate in the incident mirror (from §6an)**: the seventh picker axis is TEMPORAL-VALIDITY of the row's own premise, and the incident-mirror pipeline structurally cannot see it — the mirror stamps every main-branch CI failure as a P0-critical row the moment the run concludes, with no check on whether the failure was transient, and the a42 pick is the cost model: run 40994815953 failed on main at 02:59:32Z, the mirror filed the P0 row at 03:00:14Z (twenty minutes later — by which time the SAME commit's rerun had already gone green at 02:59:41Z, nine seconds after the failure, and the next main commit's run was green too), and the row then sat `status:unclaimed` at the top of every solver queue advertising a live production blocker that had never survived nine seconds. The agent-side half of the gate already works — the dispatch template's STALE-INCIDENT GUARD made this lane's first ci-fixer pick a skip-with-evidence instead of a claim-and-flail, and a peer lane had filed the full triage twenty minutes before that — but the row surface itself carries NO trace of any of it: no disposition label, no tier demotion, no comment, so the picker re-offers the same stale incident every dispatch and every agent re-derives the staleness from the runs surface from scratch (the incident-layer treadmill — a fixed per-dispatch tax on every fleet agent until an admin closes the row). The missing half-check lives in the MIRROR (or the watchdog), at the moment of row creation: before stamping the P0 tier, query the runs surface for a same-sha rerun and for any later main-branch run; on a green hit, stamp `disposition:transient` (the triage's own recommendation), demote the tier, and skip the solver-queue advertisement — the row still exists as the failure's record, but it stops advertising itself as unowned live work. Fallback if the mirror cannot be changed: have the label-plane batch sweep read the same two queries when it next touches the row. Cost: one runs query per failure event at mirror time (or per sweep pass), the same query the agent-side guard already runs per dispatch. This composes with item 2 (forensic metadata — the disposition stamp IS the machine-readable verdict the U3 guards currently re-derive), with item 11 (legible labels — a stale P0 label on a self-healed incident is the incident-layer instance of the stale-`status:*` family), and with item 16 (challenge-aware closure gating — both are closure/disposition paths that must read state before writing it).
19. **Gitignore-shadowed evidence trees in the evidence-plane scan (from §6ao)**: item 17's scan is only as good as its visibility, and the repo's own hygiene file hides half the evidence plane — `.gitignore` carries `reports/` (scoped "Runtime health-report outputs"), a pattern that shadows `docs/reports/` wholesale; the tree's 136 files are git-tracked (the lane's dossier, the row-level triage receipts, the verification addenda) but invisible to any ignore-respecting traversal, so an implementation of item 17 as a naive recursive scan over `docs/` false-negatives every reports-plane receipt — including the triage receipt for the very row item 18's treadmill keeps re-serving — and re-assigns completed work exactly as the scan was written to prevent. The verified live behaviors this cycle: an ignore-respecting traversal over `docs/` misses the entire tree while returning the `docs/bridge/` hits, and an explicit path argument (`docs/reports/`) serves the tree — the root-argument override IS the fix, plus the same enumeration for every scan consumer (picker, mirror, watchdog, human auditors grepping the repo for receipts). The shadow also arms a submission footgun: a filer adding a NEW file under `docs/reports/` with a plain `git add` gets a refusal ("paths are ignored") and must force the add; modifications to already-tracked files are unaffected. Cost: argument hygiene, zero new machinery. This composes with item 17 (the scan this item makes visible) and item 13 (the shared body-parse pass that feeds it); together with item 17 it closes both halves of the evidence-plane blind spot: what counts as a receipt (filename-first matching) and where receipts can be seen (explicit-root enumeration).
20. **Durable serve ledger against the fresh-process REPEAT treadmill (from §6aq)**: the loop's anti-stampede dedup — the dispatched-rows set that correctly suppresses re-offers within a session — lives only in process memory, and the cron orchestrator spawns a fresh process every dispatch, so the set dies with each cycle: a row that stays in the queue is re-served to the same lane every dispatch, each serve costing a full premise re-derivation. The a45 cycle is the third serve of #2557 to this lane and completes the first back-to-back same-row pair (a44→a45); the a44-rerun-vs-a45-loop comparison isolates the mechanism — the in-process set held the row so the rerun descended to a P3 row, the fresh process re-ranked and returned to the top — so the REPEAT class is a structural invariant of fresh-process dispatch, not a picker anomaly. The tax is the dispatch-side twin of item 18's mirror-side treadmill, heaviest exactly where the queue is most stuck: rows whose remedy is operator-plane can never exit by agent action, sit at the top of the eligible sort, and collect the tax every dispatch. The fix is a durable serve ledger the picker reads at startup: key (row, lane, dispatch-id) with a per-lane recency window, down-ranking a row served to the same lane within N cycles (N small — per-lane windows must differ so a row skipped by every eligible lane still resurfaces; the ledger must discount, not starve). Implementable Tower-side (the claim/dispatch plane already observes serves) or as a tracked ledger file the loop appends to — the loop's current runtime state file is gitignored and process-local by design, which is precisely the gap. The deeper cut composes with the queue model: rows whose remedy plane is outside the agent envelope (audit:finding + operator-plane remedy, the contract-forbidden axis) should never enter the solver-dispatch queue at all — a report-only queue (feed the evidence lanes, not the solver lane) removes the tax instead of discounting it. This composes with item 18 (the mirror-side gate — together they cover row birth and row re-serve), with item 11 (the serve ledger and the stale-label sweep read the same row state), and with item 17 (the saturation scan IS the lane-side premise re-derivation the ledger would stop repeating).
21. **Self-released report rows in the dispatch queue (from §6ar)**: the fifth row-class instance of an assignment minted for work that does not exist is the row whose body IS the deliverable — the platform sentry-pass pattern: the filer's role contract claims the row, files the pass, and releases the claim at creation ("release follows this report"), so the issue exists only as a dated receipt of a completed external-state sweep; every fact it carries is point-in-time (the a46 pick was ~49.5h old at dispatch, the oldest serve of the series), its subject is operator-plane by definition (external provider health), and its premise as a TASK is nil — the work was finished before the row was ever an assignment. The pick path items 13-17 already parse the body; the missing marker list is the filer's own lifecycle prose: self-release phrases ("release follows this report", "claim released with this filing"), role-contract pass headers (a dated "sentry pass"/"dependency verification" snapshot title), and the absence of any ask (no proposed fix, no promised artifact, no remedy marker — item 14's marker list comes back empty). On a hit, emit the evidence-classification instruction (or nothing) instead of a solver assignment — the row is already its own receipt, and a solver assignment can only mint a duplicate acknowledgment (the fifth agent's unexecuted serve on the same row, five hours stale at the a46 pre-verify, is the cost model running live). Cost: one marker scan per candidate at pick time, riding the same body-parse pass items 13-17 need (five marker lists, one scan pass). This composes with item 15 (promised artifacts — the self-released row promises nothing, so item 15's scan passes vacuously and needs this companion), with item 17 (community receipts — the pass series IS its own receipt chain), with item 20 (the report-only queue — item 21 identifies the subclass, item 20 builds the queue that should hold it), and with item 11 (the same row also rode a stale claim from an unexecuted serve — the label sweep and the pick-time scan read the same surface).

22. **Closure-time live-state probe (from §6as — the death-side twin of item 18)**: item 18 gates row BIRTH against live state (a CI-failure incident whose referenced run is superseded is demoted before it ever advertises), and this item gates row DEATH the same way: an automated or manual-sweep closure of a row whose premise is a live-service state (audit:finding on an outage, incident rows, regression reports) must run the row's own probe at closure time and close only on a green/neutral read; on a red read, the closure must stamp `disposition:still-live` and either keep the row open, re-tier it, or convert it to an operator-plane escalation — never silently mark resolved a premise that still fails. The a47 cycle is the cost model with three instruments: #2557 (the ARW outage row) was closed 07:10–07:13Z with zero writes, and three probes across three lanes — guard #3093 at 07:47Z, peer cycle probe 21 at 08:0xZ, this lane's own probe at 08:2xZ — all returned the outage signature ("SUPREMEAI_BACKEND_URL is not configured") an hour PAST the closure; #3070 (the telegram regression) closed as self-healed with the recovery unrecorded, and the channel was unreachable again 59 minutes after the last healthy bracket; #3047/#3028/#2225 closed writing nothing at all. Peer C56's sentence is the design brief: "The system closes rows against live state without checking live state." The probe is already defined for most rows — it is the same probe the filer used to create the row (the row body names it), and the batch/label-plane sweep proposed in item 11's fallback already touches the row at exactly the right moment to run it. A closure that cannot probe (no live-checkable premise, e.g. superseded report rows) is exempt — that is the case the closure-record convention already handles with written rationale. Cost: one probe per closure candidate, identical to the probe cost item 18 already pays per birth candidate; together the two items make the row lifecycle live-state-checked at BOTH ends. This composes with item 16 (closure gating — this is the concrete check the gate runs), with item 11 (a closed row with a still-live premise is the worst instance of the stale-label family — the label sweep and this probe read the same state), with item 18 (birth/death symmetric gates on one mechanism family), and with item 20 (closure is the only exit the dispatch treadmill allows, which makes the closure path's correctness load-bearing for the whole queue: a false close does not drain the premise, it just silences the row that reported it).

23. **Chain-walk completeness gate for series and batch closures (from §6at)**: the closure-record convention's sweep closed ten rows in 78 minutes and titled one of them "the LAST open 10-28-generation row" — false at landing, because the generation's newest member (#2237, the sentry series' 18:43Z pass, still open) cannot appear in ANY predecessor's chain: every pass body names only the rows that came before it, so a completeness check that derives the member set from row bodies, closure tables, or the target row's own history is STRUCTURALLY blind to the youngest member — the only instrument that sees the miss is the live pick plane, which is exactly where the fresh process's next dispatch landed (the "fully closed" generation re-served to this lane as its top candidate, the fourteenth skip). The gate: any closure record claiming generation/batch/series completeness must re-derive the member set from the live board at closure time (a title/label/series-pattern search over open rows — the same query the pick plane already runs every dispatch, which is how this miss surfaced), and on a hit either close the found member in the same record or annotate the record as partial ("n of m closed; #NNNN remains"). Cost: one board query per completeness claim. This is the row-SET twin of item 22's row-STATE probe: item 22 checks that a closed row's premise is actually dead (live-service state); item 23 checks that a closed SET actually contains everything it claims (live-row state); together with item 18 the row lifecycle gets live-state-checked at birth, death, AND set boundary. This composes with item 21 (the missed member IS a self-released report row — the marker scan would also catch it at pick time), with item 11 (the same board search feeds the stale-label sweep), and with item 16 (closure gating — the completeness check is the second check the gate runs).

## 8. Verification contract and reflection

- **3-tier contract honored on this lane's own deliverable**: (1) docs-only, zero source/CI files touched — nothing to compile, and the repo's own guards confirm main is green post-landing (run 4618 success); (2) `--verify-only` 8/8 PASS executed pre-submit at the rebased tip; (3) full `pytest tests/` on the rebased worktree: **0 failed** (counts in the PR body's evidence block). Dual-rebase on `origin/main` performed at work-start and pre-push; main moved twice during the cycle (9e6bbc9 → b4f7bb2/#1982 by a parallel lane → dbda680) and the branch carries 0 behind at submit.
- **Evidence trail**: Tower pulls all explicit-repo (`Supremeai-bd/supremeai-open`) — `github_workflow_runs` for the 4614–4618 window (note: the tool's parameter is `limit`, default 5, and large windows truncate at 512 KB — probe downward, not upward), `github_get_failed_logs` with `runId` as a **string** for run 40636808713; guard/landing/concurrency mechanics read from `.github/workflows/pipeline.yml` at the rebased tip (read-only — protected path); PR #1917's body section and state scraped from its public page; absence of #1917 commits proven over ALL fetched remote heads, not just `main`.
- **Self-correction trail (kept honest)**: the finding issue #1990 was filed with the initial hypothesis that the lander "consumed the guard-free green run"; reading the landing job's source refined this — the lander fired **from the failing run itself** because the guard is not in its `needs` (§4). The issue body also lost its inline-code fragments (backtick command-substitution during the zero-credential issue creation one-liner); the substance survived and this doc is the canonical, verbatim-receipt record for the lane. Both refinements are disclosed here rather than silently patched.
- **Reflection**: the 12:20-cycle lesson said board truth beats loop verdicts; this cycle extends it — **run-pair diffs beat single-run reads**. A "green main + green PR lane" board can still hide a constitutional gate that failed and was overridden in the same minute. The guard did its job and was structurally ignored; that combination is invisible to every existing watchdog plane (federation ingress keyed on landing-job failure; landing watchdog keyed on WO/PR/main squash reconciliation — all three saw a healthy landing). Fleet-level takeaway for lane owners: when your PR's CI history shows a red `Anti-Duplicate PR Guard` alongside a landing, do not assume a duplicate was prevented — check whether your lane shared a WO number with a lane-mate (§5), and cite this report's §7 in any follow-up.
